STAFF REPORT 
 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Amir Caus, AICP, Senior Planner 
Date of Meeting: May 27, 2025 
Type of Item: Rezone to New Mixed Use (NMU) – Master Planned Development (MPD) 
Work Session 
Process:   Legislative 
File:   25-001, 25-002, and 25-003 
 
   
 
 
Proposal 
 
The applicant is proposing to Rezone Parcel SCBP-10-2AM from the Community Commercial 
Zone to the Neighborhood Mixed-Use 1 (NMU-1) Zone. The proposal includes 64 residential 
units, ~7,700 sq. ft. commercial, and ~12,000 sq. ft. of indoor amenity space. 
 
Project Description 
 
Project Name: Latitudes MPD-NMU 
Applicant(s):   Clive Bridgwater - Bridgwater Consulting Group & Saralee Atencio 
Property Owner(s):   Fisher Creek, LLC (Roger Arsht and Ron Sharp) 
Location:   6417 N Pace Frontage Rd, Silver Summit, Summit County, UT  
Existing Zone District:   Community Commercial (CC) 
Proposed Zone District: Neighborhood Mixed-Use 1 (NMU-1) 
Parcel Number and Size: SCBP-10-2AM (8.37 acres) 
Type of Process:   Legislative (Rezone and Development Agreement)    
    Administrative (Conditional Use Permit, Low Impact Permit,  
    and Condo Plat) 
Final Land Use Authority: Summit County Council (Rezone and Development    
    Agreement) and Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
    (Conditional Use Permit, Low Impact Permit, and Final   
    Condo Plat) 
 
 
3
2 
 
Vicinity Map 
 
 
 
Background 
 
The Silver Creek Business Park Subdivision plat was originally recorded in 1997.  The plat 
currently consists of 7 lots ranging from 2.00 acres to 8.37 acres, with Lot 10 being the largest.  
An associated Landscaping Plan was recorded in 2000. There is no Development Agreement or 
Specially Planned Area associated with this property. 
 
Analysis  
 
The applicant has stated the following goals for the work session; 
 
“Our project, Latitudes, needs the assistance of the Planning Commission in order to 
cooperatively clarify a few issues: 
 
a. In order to determine whether Latitudes is viable, we need to know if we will be granted the 
60' height (five floors) we are requesting for buildings 'B' & 'C' and the higher than 32' height 
and less than 60' height for building 'A'. We feel that the Latitudes site is an ideal location for a 
five-story building because its height doesn't conflict with any neighboring properties. 
 
Analysis: As drawn, the project does not meet the height requirements set forth in the 
NMU-1 section of the Snyderville Basin Development Code.  
 
4
3 
 
 
b. Latitudes has approximately 7,700 s.f. of commercial space on the first floor of building 'A', 44 
market-rate condominiums, and 20 affordable housing units. The use of the two 
commercial spaces will not be determined until tenants are found. The number of affordable 
housing units is determined by a number of factors including what the commercial space is used 
for. The assumption has been that half the commercial space will be used for a restaurant. One-
half of the commercial space was labeled as a 'restaurant'. This label was an example of what 
the space could be used for. As I said earlier, we don't know what those spaces will be used for. 
We need the Planning Commission to determine how many units of affordable housing units 
should be part of the project based on all the contributory factors so we can move forward with 
our planning. We are not contesting the inclusion of the affordable housing requirement. We 
simply need to know how many units is the right number. 
 
Analysis: The affordable housing requirement is determined by the Snyderville Basin 
Development Code NMU-1 Zone and Chapter 5 requirements, not the Planning 
Commission.  
 
c. The multi-use designation of the project requires us to have different uses on the property. 
We currently have market-rate, affordable housing, amenity space, and commercial space. The 
question is whether we need to include commercial space in the Latitudes Project considering 
the changing composition of projects in the region. We need the Planning Commission to 
determine whether commercial space is a necessary component of the project. We do not object 
to having commercial space. We need to know whether this is a requirement or if there are 
alternative compositions we can explore. 
 
Analysis: The proposal includes 64 residential units, ~7,700 sq. ft. commercial, and 
~12,000 sq. ft. of indoor amenity space. The Planning Commission does not design 
projects or make proposals. Please refer to Section 10-2-17(A) of the Snyderville Basin 
Development Code which states the purpose of the NMU-1 Zone. 
 
d. We have submitted a report that concludes that our amenity space will generate 1/2 
employee units. The county had concerns that the amenity space might be used as commercial 
space. The amenity space is to be used exclusively by the residents and will not be used in any 
commercial capacity. We will have explicit language in our CC&Rs that dictate this requirement. 
We need to know if a rule in the CC&Rs is sufficient.” 
 
Analysis: The proposal includes ~7,700 sq. ft. commercial which has been divided in half 
for a retail and restaurant use. The proposal also includes areas that have been 
identified by the applicant as amenities. These areas include management offices, 
concierge desk, various lobby areas, sauna rooms, steam rooms, massage rooms, 
conference rooms, yoga room, gym, laundry room, various restrooms, media room, bar, 
juice bar, social areas, kitchen, greenhouse gardens, rooftop pool, and outdoor games 
and seating. Preliminary numbers show that the employee generation will be higher 
than ½ employee unit claimed by the applicant. 
 
5
4 
 
The purpose of this work session is for the applicant to introduce the project and receive initial 
feedback from the Planning Commission. Staff has listed key discussion items below which will 
help guide future analysis and meetings.  
 
Rezone 
 
The subject property is in the Community Commercial (CC) Zone. The density in the CC Zone is 
determined by the applicant’s ability to meet the minimum requirements of the zone (height, 
setbacks etc.).  The height in the CC Zone is 32 feet. To achieve higher density, the applicant has 
filed an application to rezone the property to the NMU-1 Zone. Section 10-2-17(G) of the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code states;  
 
Height: Unless otherwise permitted through the Master Planned Development (MPD) process, 
the maximum building height in the NMU-1 zone shall be forty-five feet (45'). 
 
Exception: The county council may grant a height exception up to a maximum of sixty feet 
(60') to accommodate affordable housing and/or structured parking through the MPD 
process. 
 
As drawn the maximum height is 61.5 feet and 68 feet with the mechanical 
screening/enclosure. See further analysis in Height section below.  
 
The NMU-1 Rezone process is a legislative process and therefore is discretionary. Compliance 
with the NMU-1 requirements will be required prior to any final decision.   
 
Density 
 
The applicant is requesting a height increase in order to accommodate the number of units 
proposed. Section 10-2-17(E) of the Snyderville Basin Development Code states;  
 
Density: The maximum density in the NMU-1 shall be determined by the ability of the 
proposed development to meet all required development and performance standards and 
criteria set forth in this title. 
 
The County Council has identified the need to mitigate and reduce traffic congestion in the 
Snyderville Basin as one of its priorities. The Engineering Department and Transportation 
Division will require a traffic study before they can confirm compliance. 
 
Height 
 
As drawn, the project does not meet the maximum height requirements.  
 
The NMU-1 Zone also requires that any building above 35’ in height is required to have a step 
back of 15’. The project as designed does not meet this requirement. The project will either 
need to be amended to incorporate the shift or the applicant will need to apply for a code 
amendment.  
6
5 
 
 
Affordable Housing 
 
Section 10-5-2(H.12) of the Snyderville Basin Code states; Affordable Housing: For purposes of 
the NMU-1 zone, and as a condition of receiving increased uses and densities within said zone, 
the obligation rates set forth under sections 10-5-5(A) (Residential Base Requirement) and 10-5-
6(A) (Commercial Base Requirement) (the obligation rate is also referred to as the mitigation 
rate in 10-5-6(E)) are increased to fifty percent (50%).  
 
Section 10-5-2(C.6) of the Snyderville Basin Code states; The first five thousand (5,000) square 
feet of a new commercial use; this is a onetime exemption. 
 
The applicant has identified on the plans half of the commercial space as restaurant and half as 
retail. While they have identified the two spaces, the applicant has also stated that they are not 
sure what type of business and tenant would actually occupy the place. According to the 
applicant; “The proposed commercial services and residences at Silver Creek will be a vital 
community gathering place with shops, a restaurant, meeting spaces, spa, pool, social gardens, 
gym, outdoor activity areas, forty-four luxury condominiums and twenty-, 1 and 2 bedroom 
affordable living units.”  
 
The applicant has provided an independent affordable housing study as allowed by Section 10-
5-6(C) of the Snyderville Basin Code. The study shows that the amenity spaces will generate ½ 
employee unit. Staff has found discrepancies in the study and the preliminary numbers show 
that the employee generation will be higher than ½ employee unit claimed by the applicant. 
Staff can report back at future meetings once the affordable housing study and detailed 
discussions have been finalized. 
 
General Plan 
 
The proposed project is located in the East Basin Neighborhood of the General Plan.  
 
The neighborhood description states; The East Basin neighborhood is highly visible  
from US-40 and others areas in the immediate vicinity. It is an important entry corridor into the 
Snyderville Basin and an additional entrance into Park City. 
 
The dominant features of this planning area are large tracts of relatively flat open lands, a 
stream corridor, wetlands, floodplains, and soils contaminated by mine tailings. It also contains 
areas of clustered development around the interchange of US-40 and Silver Summit, with other 
commercial uses interspersed throughout. 
The Pace Frontage Road is a popular route for bikers. 
 
The visual connectivity to existing open spaces is an important community feature and should be 
preserved as much as possible. Future design objectives that support this goal, such as locating 
structures at the outer edge of the meadow, clustering development near existing uses, and 
avoiding strip mall type patterns of development are encouraged. Protection of existing wildlife 
corridors is an important community value. 
7
6 
 
 
Consideration should be given for future mixed-use developments and flexibility in design 
standards. This may occur through TDR’s, future Code amendments and possible rezoning of 
parcels located within the neighborhood. Prior to utilizing TDRs, a neighborhood master plan 
may be developed. Future land use patterns should also be context sensitive in terms of 
infrastructure capacity. 
 
There are no existing residential uses in this planning area; however, the Silver Creek Village 
Center, which is a mixed-use development, has been approved to be located on the southeast 
quadrant of Interstate 80 and US-40. This is a large development that will have a significant 
impact on the character of the East Basin neighborhood, such as increased traffic at the US-40 
and Silver Summit interchange. 
 
The Future Land Use Map designates this property as a Mixed Residential use. 
 
Policy 2.3 of the Snyderville Basin General Plan states;  
 
“Do not approve any new entitlements beyond those permitted by the Development Code 
until such time that existing entitlements are significantly exhausted, unless the County 
legislative body first determines that: 
 
a.  a compe lling countervailing public interest, specifically identified in the General Plan 
exists and cannot be reasonably satisfied without expanding one or more 
entitlements(s); 
 
b. suc h new entitlement(s) do not simply result in an incidental benefit to the 
public interest, but rather such entitlement(s) are intended primarily to promote 
such compelling countervailing public interest; and 
 
c. any new entitlement(s) are consistent with the Neighborhood Planning Area Plans 
and the Snyderville Basin General Plan’s Future Land Use Maps, as amended.” 
 
The goal of Chapter 6: Housing Element of the Plan states: 
 
“Provide equal housing opportunities for all residents of the Basin by facilitating 
reasonable opportunities for a variety of housing, including low and moderate income 
housing.” 
 
Recommendation 
 
The applicant has requested a work session to introduce the project and get direction from the 
Planning Commission.  
  
No formal action is requested at this meeting.

Latitudes at Silver Creek 
 
As the Silver Summit area evolves, there is a growing need for both community-based services to 
be located conveniently within our neighborhood and housing for downsizing empty nesters. Lot 10 
in the Silver Creek Business Park is an ideal location for such uses. 
The proposed commercial services and residences at Silver Creek will be a vital community 
gathering place with shops, a restaurant, meeting spaces, spa, pool, social gardens, gym, outdoor 
activity areas, forty-four luxury condominiums and twenty-, 1 and 2 bedroom affordable living units. 
A transit stop on site, along with direct connections to the growing bike path system of Park City 
and immediate freeway accessibility will make this particular location ideal for connecting this 
neighborhood to the greater Pak City Community. 
A neighborhood pocket park will be created to overlook the existing wetlands, expanding the 
natural landscaped areas to include a pond, gazebo, grass lawn area and a children’s play area. By 
tying these new amenities to the bike path system, a new congregation area will emerge for social 
events, afternoon concerts, and families to interact. 
Forty Four 2- and 3-bedroom condominiums all with underground dedicated parking, individual unit 
private storage, large exterior decks, and beautiful views of the ski resorts, will bring much needed 
active senior housing to the area. Twenty additional units will be part of our affordable housing 
contribution. A three floor, amenity filled building will include social areas exclusively for the 
residents, with roof top BBQ’s and community spaces which invite social interaction between 
neighbors. 
The proposed design is made up of five unique buildings, with underground resident parking, 
extensive landscaping, convenient visitor surface parking, and beautiful pedestrian areas that will 
create a much-needed set of social gathering spaces for the community, including a year-round 
planted conservatory. 
A local restaurant, with great outdoor terraces, will allow beautiful views into the gardens and new 
places to meet friends and family. 
Latitudes as Silver Creek will incorporate sustainable systems like rainwater collection, graywater 
irrigation, drought tolerant planting, solar collection and geothermal snow melt. 
A new green live-work-play environment will be created for a vibrant 24-hour community helping 
other local residents, merchants, and services grow and benefit. 
EXHIBIT A.1
10
 
 
Code Compliance Letter Lot 10 Silver Creek Business Park 
 
Master Plan Development Ordinance 
 
E.12, A: Natural Environment: 
This Project will create new landscaped areas that include drought tolerant local 
planting. A pocket park with expanded retention pond will collect all water from 
impervious areas of the stie. The project will increase biodiversity in the area and create 
new shaded areas to lower ambient temperatures. 
 
B: All impervious surface runoff will be drained into a retention pond, creating a new 
habitat for wildlife and migrating birds. The entire development will be supplied by 
Mountain Regional and will be connected to Snyderville Basin reclamation. High 
efficiency irrigation systems on smart control will be incorporated throughout the entire 
project. 
 
C: The project will use high efficiency mechanical systems to reduce negative impacts on 
air quality. Units will have openable windows to promote natural ventilation. 
 
D: N/A 
E: This project will use high efficiency mechanical systems, openable windows, smart 
irrigation systems, natural shading and passive solar design. It will use high efficiency 
windows and doors and super insulation; energy conservation and energy recovery 
systems will be employed. We expect to be LEED certified. 
 
F: The project will incorporate best practices in terms of both material selection and 
installation. Recycled materials will be incorporated both on the exterior and interior 
whenever possible. Pavers made from recycled content will be incorporated. 
 
G: The project will have both trash and recycling programs on-site. Individual trash 
shoots in each building per floor allow for efficient disposal of waste with street access 
for pick up by services provider. Active recycling programs for both the residences and 
commercial space will be implemented.  
EXHIBIT A.2
11
 
 
H: The project’s overall design incorporates multiple buildings oriented to allow for a 
combination of sunny and shady spaces. Roofed private and semi-private terraces create 
shade in summer but allow sun penetration in winter. 
All units have openable windows and doors to allow natural cross ventilation. The 
project has a loop driveway that is ideal for both emergency vehicles and snow removal. 
There are 7 different snow retention areas to ease plowing. All roof drains and surface 
runoff will be retained in an expanded retention pond, becoming a year-round 
community amenity. Local materials will be used when possible and construction 
recycling programs will be incorporated.  
I: The landscape design will use local drought tolerant planting and fir and aspen trees to 
create a new park like setting attracting local and migratory birds. A sophisticated water 
wise control system for the irrigation will be incorporated. Maximum pervious areas will 
be incorporated to support rain and snowmelt to be percolated back into the ground. 
 
13, A 
1) There are (3) vehicular entrances to the project from the frontage road. A loop road 
runs around the entire project and provides (25) visitor parking spaces. 
2) Multiple bike and pedestrian paths run through and around the entire property and tie 
directly into the existing bike path system at the street. 
3) Bike and pedestrian paths link to new pocket park also. 
 
B: There is a school across the street with multiple commercial properties within the 
business park and adjacent parcels. The new pocket park and expanded retention pond 
creates a new social space for the local community. 
 
C: The project brings (44) much needed 2 bedroom condos to the Summit County area as 
well as (20) affordable 1,2,& 3 bedroom units. 
 
D: The project brings new social spaces to the community, including a pocket park, 
amphitheater, pond, bike paths, gardens and 2 commercial spaces facing the street to 
become a community hub. Amenities for the active residents include a spa, gym, meeting / 
work spaces, social bar & lounges, a conservatory, social gardens, year round pool and hot 
tub, boccie ball courts and BBQ roof terraces. 
 
EXHIBIT A.3
12
 
E: The project is built into the gentle slope of the site to reduce overall height. The buildings 
step from 2,3,4 & 5 stories to break up the massing and create visual interest, The buildings 
maximize the views from the site. 
F: The project includes a transit stop at the street with an e-bike station as well as integral 
bike/pedestrian paths that connect to existing trails. The site circulation as a loop road 
creates clear route finding and ideal life safety access to all buildings. 
G: The project creates (92) subterranean parking stalls for the Required condominium 
parking (1 per bedroom) with lockable bike storage and individual ski storage. Also 
proposed are (25) visitor spaces located all around the loop road for ease of access. (24) 
additional reserved carport spaces for the affordable units are also provided. 
H: The project supplies a new neighborhood pocket park with play area, pond, gazebo, lawn 
gathering areas plus new covered outdoor terrace for the commercial spaces facing the 
street for street activation. No fences allow wildlife access through the property. 
I: The project has trash/recycling in all buildings with trash shoots per floor, with street 
access for pickup. All utilities will be subterranean with ample snow retention areas 
throughout the property. Mail/packaging rooms are located in each building for residents’ 
convenience. 
J: The project brings a series of large and small gathering spaces for both the local 
community to enjoy and also the residents. All residences have large exterior terraces and 
light filled spaces for optimal health and wellbeing. Extensive landscaped areas allow both 
residents and the local community multiple areas to enjoy nature.

CODE REVIEW
NOTES:
1. CONTRACTOR TO OBTAIN APPROVAL FROM THE APPROPRIATE FIRE DISTRICT, WATER DISTRICT, SEWER DISTRICT,
SNYDERVILLE BASIN RECREATION DISTRICT AND SUMMIT COUNTY HEALTH DEPARTMENT FOR FINAL APPROVAL OF SEWER,
WATER, WELLS AND SEPTIC SYSTEMS PRIOR TO PERMIT. COORDINATE WITH ARCHITECT AS NEEDED.
2. PRIOR TO THE INITIATION OF RESIDENTIAL DESIGN, EACH LOT BUYER SHALL RETAIN A CIVIL AND/OR GEOTECHNICAL
ENGINEER FOR LOT-AND DRIVEWAY-SPECIFIC GRADING, DRAINAGE, AND GEOTECHNICAL DESIGN.  ALL GRADING SHALL BE
DESIGNED AND CONDUCTED SO AS TO IMPLEMENT ALL SUMMIT COUNTY REQUIREMENTS
3. RESIDENTIAL STRUCTURAL DEVELOPMENT MUST OCCUR WITHIN THE BUILDING ENVELOPE DESCRIBED ON HOA APPROVED
SITE PLAN AND AS PER SUMMIT COUNTY.
4. THE DRIVEWAY SHALL CONFORM TO SUMMIT COUNTY AND FIRE AUTHORITY REGULATIONS.  THE MAXIMUM AVERAGE GRADE
OF THE FIRST 20'-0" SHALL NOT EXCEED 5%. THE MAXIMUM AVERAGE GRADE OF ANY DRIVEWAY, BEYOND THE FIRST 20'-0"
SHALL NOT EXCEED 10%. UP TO 14% MAY BE ALLOWED WHEN APPROVED BY THE CITY ENGINEER.
5. IN CONSTRUCTION AREAS TO BE RE-VEGETATED WHERE THE SUBSOIL LAYER HAS BEEN COMPACTED, RIPPING SHALL BE
CONDUCTED DURING FINAL STAGES TO LOOSEN SOIL ALLOWING FOR BETTER SUBSURFACE DRAINAGE AND ROOT
PENETRATION.
6. THE PLACEMENT OF SOLID FENCING OR OTHER BARRIER FEATURES ALONG THE PERIMETER OF RESIDENTIAL LOTS SHALL BE
PROHIBITED.
7. THE BUYER MAY NEED TO INSTALL A PRESSURE REDUCING VALVE IN THE SERVICE LINE IN ORDER TO REDUCE RESIDENTIAL
SERVICE PRESSURE TO A MAXIMUM OF 60 POUNDS PER SQUARE INCH.
8. LOTS EQUIPPED WITH A GRAVITY SEWER SYSTEM SERVICE SHOULD BE ABLE TO PROVIDE GRAVITY SEWER TO ALL FINISHED
FLOORS AT OR ABOVE NATURAL GROUND.  HOWEVER, IT IS THE RESPONSIBILITY OF THE LOT BUYER TO VERIFY WHETHER AN
EJECTOR PUMP SEWER SERVICE WILL BE REQUIRED FOR THEIR SPECIFIC HOME DESIGN.
9. THE BUILDING AREA MAY BE ADDITIONALLY RESTRICTED BY ARCHITECTURAL REVIEW TO MINIMIZE DISTURBANCE OF ROCK
OUTCROPPINGS, SIGNIFICANT VEGETATION AND NATURAL SWALES.
10. RETAINING WALLS MORE THAN 4 FT. HEIGHT OR SUPPORTING A SURCHARGE, SWIMMING POOLS, SOLAR & GEO-THERMAIL
HEATING SYSTEMS, & PHOTO-VOLTAIC & WIND GENERATED ELECTRICAL SYSTEMS REQUIRE SEPARATE PERMITS. SEPARATE
PLANS, SPECIFICATIONS, & APPLICATIONS ARE TO BE SUBMITTED & REVIEWED PRIOR TO ISSUANCE OF THESE PERMITS.
11. SEE GENERAL NOTES A0.2 - A0.4 FOR ADDITIONAL REQUIREMENTS
12. CLOTHES DRYER NAIL PLATES SHALL PROTECT DUCT FROM BEING PENETRATED WHERE DUCT IS CONCEALED WITHIN WALLS.
A PERMANENT TAG SHALL BE ATTACHED TO VISIBLE PORTION OF DUCT IDENTIFYING LENGTH OF DUCT.
13. LAYOUTS THAT DIVIDE BATHROOMS INTO SEPARATE AREAS W/ WATER USING FIXTURES REQUIRE INDIVIDUAL VENTILATION IN
EACH OF THOSE AREAS.
14. WHERE GAS APPLIANCES ARE LOCATED  WITHIN THE GARAGE, THE GAS CONNECTION SHALL BE RAISED 18" ABOVE FINISH
FLOOR AND APPLIANCE SHALL BE PROTECTED FROM IMPACT.
15. WHERE AN OPERABLE WINDOW IS MORE THAN 72" ABOVE THE GRADE & 24" OR LESS OFF THE INTERIOR FLOOR, THERE SHALL
BE GUARDS THAT DO NOT ALLOW A SPHERE OF MORE THAN 4" TO PASS.
16. ALL LUMBER IN CONTACT WITH CONCRETE OR MASONRY INCLUDING LEDGERS AND FURRING WALLS MUST BE
PRESERVATIVELY TREATED OR FOUNDATION-GRADE REDWOOD.
17. NO WOOD SHALL BE NEARER THAN 8 INCHES TO EARTH UNLESS SEPARATED BY CONCRETE AT LEAST 3 IN. IN THICKNESS
WITH AN IMPERVIOUS MEMBRANE INSTALLED BETWEEN THE EARTH AND THE CONCRETE. THIS INCLUDES DECKS AND SIDING.
18. PROVIDE 1/2" AIRPACE AT TOPS, SIDES AND ENDS OF GIRDERS ENTERING EXTERIOR CONCRETE OR MASONRY WALL UNLESS
WOOD RESISTANT TO DECAY IS USED.
19. DECORATIVE SHROUDS SHALL NOT BE INSTALLED AT THE TERMINATION OF FACTORY BUILT CHIMNEYS EXCEPT WHERE THE
SHROUDS ARE LISTED AND LABELED FOR USE WITH THE SPECIFIC CHIMNEY SYSTEM AND INSTALLED ACCORDING TO
MANUFACTURER'S INSTRUCTIONS.
20. AS PER IRC 806.4, UNVENTED ATTIC & CATHEDRAL CEILING ROOF ASSEMBLIES, THESE CONDITIONS SHALL BE MET:
20.1. UNVENTED CATHEDRAL CEILING IS CONTAINED WITHIN BLDG. ENVELOPE.
20.2. NO INTERIOR VAPOR RETARDER SHALL BE INSTALLED ON CEILING SIDE OF INSULATION SYSTEM.
20.3. CLOSED CELL FOAM ACTS AS VAPOR RETARDER.
20.4. AIR IMPERMEABLE INSULATION (CLOSED CELL FOAM) SHALL BE APPLIED DIRECTLY TO UNDER SIDE OF ROOF SHEATHING.
21. GAS PIPING:
21.1. PROHIBITED LOCATIONS: GAS PIPING SHALL NOT BE INSTALLED IN OR THROUGH A DUCTED SUPPLY, RETURN, EXHAUST,
CLOTHES CHUTE, CHIMNEY, DUMBWAITER, OR ELEVATOR SHAFT. GAS PIPING INSTALLED DOWNSTREAM OF THE POINT OF
DELIVERY SHALL NOT EXTEND THROUGH ANY TOWNHOUSE UNIT OTHER THAN THE UNIT SERVED BY SUCH PIPING.
21.2. GAS PIPING SHALL NOT PENETRATE BUILDING FOUNDATION WALLS AT ANY POINT BELOW GRADE
21.3. GAS PIPING INSTALLED UNDERGROUND BENEATH BUILDINGS IS PROHIBITED EXCEPT WHERE THE PIPING IS ENCASED IN
A CONDUIT. SUCH CONDUIT SHALL EXTEND NOT LESS THAN 4" OUTSIDE THE BUILDING, SHALL BE VENTED ABOVE GRADE
TO THE OUTDOORS AND SHALL BE INSTALLED SO AS TO PREVENT THE ENTRANCE OF WATER OR INSECTS.
21.4. APPLIANCES SHALL NOT BE LOCATED IN SLEEPING ROOMS, BATHROOMS, TOILET ROOMS, STORAGE ROOM OR A SPACE
THAT OPENS INTO SUCH ROOMS. SEE IRC - G2406.2 FOR EXCEPTIONS.
22. DEFERRED SUBMITTALS:
22.1. RETAINING WALLS HIGHER THAN 4 FT. OR SUPPORTING A SURCHARGE REQUIRE SEPARATE PERMITS. SEPARATE PLANS,
SPECIFICATIONS, AND APPLICATIONS ARE TO BE SUBMITTED AND REVIEWED PRIOR TO ISSUANCE OF THESE PERMITS.
22.2. CONTRACTOR TO ARRANGE A MEETING WITH THE PLAN CHECKER, THE GENERAL CONTRACTOR AND THE EXCAVATOR
FOR THIS PROJECT PRIOR TO ANY PERMITS BEING ISSUED.
22.3. ROCK WALLS REQUIRED ENGINEERING.
22.4. CONTRACTOR TO PROVIDE A COPY OF THE "APPLICATION TO WORK IN A PUBLIC RIGHT OF WAY" THAT HAS BEEN
APPROVED BY THE CITY ENGINEERING DEPARTMENT.
22.5. CONTRACTOR TO PROVIDE A FIRE SPRINKLER SYSTEM PER SUMMIT COUNTY
22.6. CONTRACTOR TO PROVIDE A RADIANT HEAT SYSTEM SUBMITTAL FOR SUMMIT COUNTY APPROVAL.
22.7. CONTRACTOR TO PROVIDE A GAS PIPING SCHEMATIC FOR SUMMIT COUNTY APPROVAL. GAS PIPING PLAN AND SUMMIT
COUNTY GAS LINE INSTALLATION FORM SHALL BE ON SITE FOR GAS LINE AND METER INSPECTION.
22.8. CONTRACTOR TO PROVIDE FACTORY-BUILT FIREPLACE PRODUCT INFORMATION AND FIREPLACE LISTINGS SHOWN ON
THE PLANS AT MECHANICAL INSPECTION. PROVIDE EPA COMPLIANCE FOR WOOD BURNING FIREPLACES.
22.9. CONTRACTOR TO PROVIDE A LANDSCAPE PLAN FOR SUMMIT COUNTY APPROVAL.
22.10. SEPARATE PLANS, SPECIFICATIONS AND APPLICATIONS ARE TO BE SUBMITTED TO SUMMIT COUNTY FOR REVIEW AND
APPROVAL OF RETAINING WALLS (SEE 22.1), SWIMMING POOLS, SOLAR AND GEO-THERMAL HEATING SYSTEMS AND
PHOTO-VOLTAIC AND WIND GENERATED ELECTRICAL SYSTEMS.
22.11. EXHAUST HOOD SYSTEMS CAPABLE OF EXHAUSTING IN EXCESS OF 400 CFM SHALL BE PROVIDED WITH MAKE-UP AIR
EQUAL TO THE EXHAUST RATE. SUCH SYSTEMS SHALL BE PROVIDED WITH A DAMPER WHICH IS AUTOMATICALLY
INTERLOCKED WITH THE HOOD.
22.12. CONTRACTOR TO COORDINATE ELEVATOR SPECIAL INSPECTION WITH SUMMIT COUNTY
22.13. CONTRACTOR TO PROVIDE STEAM SHOWER DETAILS FOR SUMMIT COUNTY APPROVAL
22.14. CONTRACTOR TO PROVIDE BACKFLOW PREVENTERS FOR LAWN SPRINKLER SYSTEM, FIRE SPRINKLER SYSTEM, SNOW
MELT BOILER, HOT WATER BOILER, ETC,. AND COORDINATE APPROVAL WITH SUMMIT COUNTY
22.15. CONTRACTOR TO OBTAIN APPROVAL FROM DEPUTY FIRE MARSHAL TO INSTALL FIRE PIT.
22.16. A CERTIFICATE OF SURVEY WILL BE REQUIRED AFTER THE FOUNDATION HAS BEEN POURED AND PRIOR TO A
SHEARWALL/4-WAY INSPECTION.
22.17. CERTIFICATE OF ELEVATION: THE ELEVATIONS AT THE TOP OF FOUNDATION AT FOUR (4) MAJOR CORNERS OF ROOF
RIDGES IS REQUIRED AFTER THE FOUNDATION HAS BEEN POURED AND PRIOR TO A SHEARWALL/4-WAY INSPECTION.
23. CONTRACTOR SHALL FOLLOW AIR LEAKAGE PRESCRIPTIVE  METHOD PER N1102.4.1.1
MIN. OPENING AREA:  (SECTION 310.1.1)
ALL EMERGENCY ESCAPE AND RESCUE OPENINGS
SHALL HAVE A MIN. NET CLEAR OPENING OF 5.7 SQ'

EXHIBIT A.53
62
10-2-17: NEIGHBORHOOD MIXED-USE 1 (NMU-1) ZONE:
   A.   Purpose:
      1.   The purpose of the 
Neighborhood Mixed-Use 1 (NMU-1) zone is to create new
development and redevelopment areas that are:
         a.   Mixed use;
         b.   Pedestrian-focused; and
         c.   Transit-oriented.
      2.   The desired development pattern of the NMU-1 zone shall be facilitated through flexible site
and community design principles that:
         a.   Result in a mix of land uses that are compatible, mutually supportive, well connected
through a variety of transportation choices;
         b.   Result in convenient, safe, direct, and well-connected transit and pedestrian linkages;
         c.   Result in a reduction in the need for parking;
         d.   Result in compact development that creates public spaces, including but not limited to
plazas and other outdoor amenity areas; and
         e.   Result in sustainable infrastructure and building design consistent with best practices.
   B.   Uses: Uses in the NMU-1 zone are limited to the following:
      1.   Allowed Uses:
         a.   Bars, taverns, clubs;
         b.   Home based business, Class 1 (see Section 10-8-4);
         c.   Mixed use structure;
         d.   Mobile food business;
         e.   Open space;
         f.   Park, community;
         g.   Park, neighborhood;
         h.   Recycling facilities, Class 1;
         i.   Solar array, minor;
         j.   Telecommunication facilities, co-location;
         k.   Trails, community wide;
         l.   Trails, neighborhood;
         m.   Trailhead parking;
         n.   Offices, general; and
         o.   Retail sales, convenience store.

         b.   Commercial event center;

      4.   Temporary Uses:
         a.   Temporary use or structure.
   C.   Rezoning Eligibility: Properties must meet all the following criteria to be eligible for
consideration for a rezone to IMMU-1:
      1.   The property(s) must be designated for mixed-use on the Snyderville Basin General Plan,
Future Land Use Map;
      2.   The property(s) must be adjacent to (or a redevelopment of) existing commercial, mixed-
use, or institutional/civic development:
      3.   The property(s) must be located along an existing transit system or within the County
Master Planned Development process, receive a written commitment to provide service from the
County Regional Transportation Planning Director.
      4.   Property subject to the terms and conditions of any prior development agreement,
settlement agreement or similar instrument shall remain subject to those terms and conditions until
such time as the prior agreement is amended or terminated as part of the Master Planned
Development Process.

EXHIBIT B.3
65
   D.   Rezoning And Master Planned Development: A proposal for a rezone to the NMU-1 zone
shall be processed concurrently with a master planned development application subject to this title.
   E.   Density: The maximum density in the NMU-1 shall be determined by the ability of the
proposed development to meet all required development and performance standards and criteria
set forth in this title.
   F.   Setbacks:
      1.   Unless otherwise permitted through the Master Planned Development process, setbacks in
the NMU-1 zone shall be as follows:
Exterior Boundary Setback
To mitigate negative impacts, preserve view corridors or
create a compatible street design/streetscape, the
planning commission or county council may modify the
setbacks around the exterior boundary of the project. In
some cases, that setback may be increased to create an
adequate buffer to adjacent uses at the discretion of the
county. The planning commission/county council may
reduce setbacks within the project from those otherwise
required provided the project meets minimum
International Building Code and Fire Code requirements
and advances the goals set forth in the General Plan.
Front Setback
The minimum front setback is twenty feet (20') for all
structures. The twenty foot (20') front setback may be
reduced to ten feet (10'), provided all on-site parking is at
the rear of the property or is in structured parking.
Front Setback (if property line
extends to the centerline of a road)
The minimum front setback is forty-five feet (45') for all
structures. The forty-five foot (45') front setback may be
reduced to twenty feet (20'), provided all on-site parking is
at the rear of the property or is in structured parking. All
structures and improvements, excluding driveways, are to
be fully contained on the lot.
Side Setback The minimum side setback is ten feet (10').
Rear Setback The minimum rear setback is ten feet (10').
Wetland Setback Forty feet (40') from delineation line as defined by the
Army Corps of Engineers.
East Canyon Creek Setback One hundred fifty feet (150') from centerline.
River or Perennial Stream Setback One hundred feet (100') from centerline.
Lake or Natural Pond Setback Fifty feet (50') from high water mark.
Designated Roadway Setback,
Highways 224, 40, 248, and
Interstate 80

      2.   On a corner lot, one minimum front setback and one minimum side setback is required with
the following provisions:
         a.   Any frontage with a driveway leading to a garage or parking space shall have a front

         b.   On any corner lot, a clear view area must be maintained. This is a triangular area formed

from the intersection of the property lines. No obstruction to view more than three (3) feet in height
shall be placed in the clear view area, including walls, fences, structures, signs, trees, shrubs, or
hedges. When topography presents a clear view, the area shall be graded to provide visual
clearance.
   G.   Height: Unless otherwise permitted through the Master Planned Development (MPD)
process, the maximum building height in the NMU-1 zone shall be forty-five feet (45').
   Exception: The county council may grant a height exception up to a maximum of sixty feet (60') to
accommodate affordable housing and/or structured parking through the MPD process.
   For any building sharing a common property line with a detached single-family dwelling unit, the
maximum height is limited to forty-five feet (45') and may not be increased through any process.
   Step Back Requirement: Floors rising above thirty-five feet (35') in height shall be stepped back
fifteen (15) horizontal feet from the building foundation at grade for building elevations that are
facing a public street, public trail, or public open space. This step back does not apply to balconies
on buildings with floors rising above thirty-five feet (35') in height.
   H.   Performance Standards: In addition to compliance with all Master Planned Development
criteria set forth in this title, the following performance criteria shall apply to the NMU-1 zone.
      1.   Open Space: Unless otherwise approved through the Master Planned Development
process, all Master Planned Developments in the NMU-1 zone shall provide twenty-five percent
(25%) of the project area for outdoor space, plaza, pathways, and/or trails and open space.
      2.   Mixed Use: Unless otherwise approved through the MPD process, all Master Planned
Developments in the NMU-1 zone shall have a minimum of three (3) land uses (residential,
commercial, office, recreation, civic, etc.) integrated into the project area. A range of different uses
along the street, as well as diversity in building design and scale is encouraged wherever
practicable.
      3.   Limitation On Direct Retail Sales: Unless otherwise approved through the Master Planned
Development process, no single retail use may exceed fifty thousand (50,000) square feet.
      4.   Single-Story Commercial Building Standards: Unless otherwise approved through the
Master Planned Development process, no single-story commercial structures greater than one
thousand (1,000) square feet are permitted in the NMU-1 zone.
      5.   Access To Public Transportation: All Master Planned Developments in the NMU-1 zone
shall provide facilities that substantially facilitate, enhance, and support the use of alternative public
transportation options such as transit services, neighborhood circulators, employee shuttles, car
share, bicycle share, and bicycle and pedestrian infrastructure. Unless otherwise permitted through
the Master Planned Development process, all Master Planned Developments in the NMU-1 zone
shall provide a transit center, transportation stops and/or other improvements necessary to mitigate
the impacts of the development. Unless otherwise permitted through the Master Planned

               (E)   Carpool spaces;
               (F)   Covered bike parking;
               (G)   Site location as it relates to transit;
               (H)   Shared parking;
               (I)   Other conclusions from the parking study; and
               (J)   The change in parking shall not result in adverse impacts to public walkways, plazas,
or other pedestrian circulation areas.

            (1)   Unless otherwise permitted through the Master Planned Development process, off-

      9.   Architecture And Building Construction: The project architecture shall foster a distinct
neighborhood character. Designs shall be sensitive to the landscape and topography of the site.
While new structures are not required to mimic historic structures or themed designs, buildings in
the NMU-1 zone should reflect local and regional practices regarding materials, and roof forms.
Streetscapes should respond to the human, pedestrian scale rather than the automobile.
Architecture that relies on standardized corporate designs, mechanical climate control and
automobile accessibility is strongly discouraged.
      10.   Sustainabilitv: Projects shall be socially, economically, and environmentally sustainable.
Buildings should be designed, oriented, and constructed to maximize the potential for improved
efforts regarding:
         a.   Connectivity;
         b.   Energy efficiency;
         c.   On-site energy generation;
         d.   Solar orientation, natural light, and ventilation;
         e.   Thermal and light reduction;
         f.   Sustainable materials; and
         g.   Water conservation landscaping such as:
            (1)   Drought tolerant plant species;
            (2)   Native plant species;
            (3)   Xeriscaping;
            (4)   Time of day watering; and
            (5)   Rain or moisture sensors on irrigation systems.

10-2-18: MASTER PLANNED DEVELOPMENTS:
   A.   Intent: A Master Planned Development (MPD) is a comprehensive project design strategy.
MPDs are intended to provide better opport
unities to create projects that address site conditions,
the characteristics of the surrounding properties, as well as community and market demands. The
MPD process creates administrative tools to promote the efficient use of land resources as well as
efficient public infrastructure and utility services. The goal of this strategy is to produce superior
project design through flexibility and innovation to advance the goals of the Snyderville Basin
General Plan and this code.
   B.   Applicability:
   Required: Unless the property is subject to an approved development agreement, settlement
agreement or consent agreement, the MPD process shall be required in all zones for the following:
      1.   Any application for a rezone;
      2.   A residential project with ten (10) or more units of density; and
      3.   Any new hotel or lodging project, commercial, retail, office, public, quasi-public, mixed use,
or industrial project with ten thousand (10,000) square feet or more of gross floor area.
   C.   Uses: An MPD can only contain uses, which are allowed, low impact or conditional in the
existing zone district(s) in which it is located. When the project area includes more than one (1)
zone district, uses may be relocated across zone district lines if the planning commission
determines that relocation results in a project design that advances the goals of the Snyderville
Basin General Plan.
   D.   Process:
      1.   Pre-Application Conference: A pre-application conference shall be held with staff for the
applicant to become acquainted with the MPD procedures and related county requirements and
schedules. Staff may give preliminary feedback to the applicant based on information available at
the conference and may inform the applicant of potential issues or special requirements which may
result from the proposal. Any direction or feedback given at the pre-application conference is not
vested. Vesting occurs once a complete application is filed, and the project has been reviewed and
approved by the county council or planning commission.
      2.   Pre-Application Work Session Public Meeting:
         a.   To provide an opportunity for the public and the planning commission to give preliminary
input on a concept for an MPD, the applicant may request a work session discussion with the
planning commission after the pre-application conference with staff. Any direction or feedback
given at the pre-application work session public meeting is not vested. Vesting occurs once a
complete application is filed, and the project has been reviewed and approved by the county
council or planning commission.
         b.   At the pre-application work session public meeting, the applicant will have an opportunity
to present the preliminary concepts for the proposed MPD. The public will be given an opportunity
to comment on the preliminary concepts so that the applicant can address neighborhood concerns
in preparation of an application for an MPD.
         c.   It is recommended that the applicant host neighborhood meetings in preparation of filing
of a formal application for an MPD and provide a report conveying neighborhood concerns
discussed.

EXHIBIT C.1
71
      3.   The MPD Application:
         a.   Plans for the MPD shall be submitted with a completed application form supplied by the
county. A list of minimum requirements will accompany the application form. The application must
include written consent by all owners of the property to be included in the MPD. Once an
application is received, it shall be assigned to a staff planner who will review the application for
completeness. The applicant will be informed if additional information is necessary to constitute a
complete application.
         b.   Planning Commission Review And Public Hearing: Except in the case of MPDs that
include a rezone, the planning commission shall approve, approve with modifications, or deny an
MPD. The planning commission action shall be in the form of written findings of fact, conclusions of
law, and in the case of approval, conditions of approval. Action shall occur only after public hearing
is held. To approve an MPD, the planning commission will be required to make the findings outlined
in section F herein. In the case of MPDs that include a rezone, the planning commission shall
review and forward a recommendation to the county council, including findings of fact, conclusions
of law and conditions of approval.
         c.   County Council Review And Public Hearing: In the case of MPDs that include a rezone,
the county council shall approve, approve with modifications, or deny an MPD. The county council
action shall be in the form of written findings of fact, conclusions of law and in the case of approval,
conditions of approval. Action shall occur only after a public hearing is held. To approve an MPD,
the county council will be required to make the findings outlined in section F herein.
      4.   Development Agreement:
         a.   Once the planning commission or county council approves a Master Planned
Development, the approval shall be put in the form of a development agreement reviewed by the
county attorney's office and shall contain, at a minimum, the following:
            (1)   A legal description of the land;
            (2)   All relevant zoning and development code parameters, including all findings,
conclusions, and conditions of approval, specifying any exceptions outlining more or less restrictive
height or setbacks;
            (3)   An express reservation of the future legislative power and zoning authority of the
county;
            (4)   A copy of the approved site plan, architectural plans, landscaping plans, grading plan,
trails and open space plans, and other plans, which are a part of the approval;
            (5)   A description of all developer exactions or agreed upon public dedications;
            (6)   The developers agreement to pay all specified impact fees;
            (7)   The form of ownership anticipated for the project; and
            (8)   A specific project phasing plan.
         b.   The development agreement shall be signed by the chair of either the planning
commission or the chair of the county council and recorded with the Summit County recorder. The
development agreement shall contain language to allow for minor, administrative modifications
without revision of the agreement. The development agreement shall be reviewed and signed as
part of the final approval by the planning commission or county council.

      5.   Vesting Of Approval:
         a.   MPDs Not Associated With A Final Subdivision Plat: Construction within the MPD area
will be required to commence within five (5) years of the date of the MPD approval. After
construction commences, the MPD shall remain valid if it is consistent with the approved specific
project-phasing plan as set forth in the approved final site plan and associated documents. It is
anticipated that the specific project-phasing plan may require review and re-evaluation of the
project at specified points in the development of the project to be described in the development
agreement.
         b.   MPDs Not Associated With A Rezone But Requiring A Final Subdivision Plat: A final
subdivision plat must be recorded within five (5) years of the date of the MPD approval.
Additionally, construction within the MPD area will be required to commence within five (5) years of
the date of the MPD approval. If the required final subdivision plat recordation and construction
commencement has not taken place in the prescribed timeframe, the MPD shall expire and the final
subdivision plat shall be void.
         c.   MPDs Associated With A Rezone, But Not Requiring A Final Subdivision Plat:
Construction within the MPD area will be required to commence within five (5) years of the date of
the MPD approval. After construction commences, the MPD shall remain valid if it is consistent with
the approved specific project-phasing plan as set forth in the approved final site plan and
associated documents. It is anticipated that the specific project-phasing plan may require review
and re-evaluation of the project at specified points in the development of the project. If the required
construction commencement has not taken place in the prescribed timeframe, the MPD shall expire
and the zone shall revert to the previous zone designation.
         d.   MPDs Associated With A Rezone And Final Subdivision Plat: Unless otherwise extended
per the provisions set forth in this chapter, a final subdivision plat associated with a rezone must be
recorded within five (5) years of the date of the MPD approval. Additionally, construction within the
MPD area will be required to commence within five (5) years of the date of the MPD approval. If the
required final subdivision plat recordation and construction commencement has not taken place in
the prescribed timeframe, the MPD shall expire and the zone shall revert to the previous zone
designation.
      After recordation of the final subdivision plat and the commencement of construction, the MPD
shall remain valid if it is consistent with the approved specific project plan and associated
documents.
      6.   MPD Modifications:
         a.   Minor Amendment: A minor amendment is defined as an amendment that does not
increase square footage, density, or intensity of the previously approved MPD. A minor amendment
shall be processed as a low impact permit.
         b.   Major Amendment: A major amendment is defined as an amendment that increases
square footage, density, or intensity of the previously approved MPD. A major amendment shall be
processed as an MPD.
   E.   MPD Requirements: All MPD applications shall meet the following minimum requirements.
Additional project information necessary for the project analysis may be required at the discretion of
the community development director, planning commission, or county council.
      1.   Affected Properties: All MPD applications shall include all contiguous holdings by the owner,
unless specifically waived by the planning commission. The rezoning application shall be
accompanied by an affirmation of ownership signed by the legal owner of the property. If the
Firefox https://export.amlegal.com/api/export-requests/d4ff7bee-f51a-41fe-be7...

EXHIBIT C.3
73
property is under contract for sale, the affirmation shall be signed by the contract owner and legal
owner and specify the date a contract of sale was executed. In the event corporations are involved,
a copy of the resolution legally empowering the applicant to make application shall be provided with
the application.
      2.   Density:
         a.   The type of development, number of units and density permitted on a given site will be
determined through a site suitability analysis and shall not exceed the maximum density in the
zone, except as otherwise provided in this section. The site shall be looked at in its entirety and the
density located in the most appropriate locations.
         b.   In cases where a project site contains more than one (1) zone district, the planning
commission may permit a shift of density between zone districts if the shift results in the project
advancing the goals set forth in the general plan.
         c.   Square footage associated with underground/structured parking areas within an MPD is
exempt from MPD density calculations.
         d.   Square footage associated with deed-restricted affordable housing units within an MPD is
exempt from MPD density calculations.
      3.   Setbacks: Setbacks for all structures within an MPD shall be determined by the
requirements found in each individual zone district.
         a.   Exception: To mitigate negative impacts, preserve view corridors or create a compatible
street design/streetscape, the planning commission or county council may modify the setbacks
around the exterior boundary of the project. In some cases, that setback may be increased to
create an adequate buffer to adjacent uses at the discretion of the county. The planning
commission/county council may reduce setbacks within the project from those otherwise required
provided the project meets minimum International Building Code and Fire Code requirements and
advances the goals set forth in the general plan.
      4.   Building Height: The maximum building height for all structures within an MPD shall not
exceed the requirements found in each individual zone district.
      5.   Site Planning: An MPD shall be designed to take into consideration characteristics of the
site upon which it is proposed to be placed. The project should be designed to fit the site, not the
site modified to fit the project. The following shall be addressed in the site planning for an MPD:
         a.   Designing With The Topography: MPDs shall be designed to fit into the topography of the
site. The planning commission/county council may consider flexibility in the siting of the
development to best fit into the natural terrain, minimize excessive site grading and the need for
excessive retaining.
      Efforts shall be made to mitigate impacts of the development on the natural environment and
resources of the surrounding area. The project design shall make suitable provisions for the
preservation of all affected critical lands.
         b.   Designing With Adjacent Uses: The MPD site plan shall take adjacent land uses into
consideration. Development along the project perimeter shall adequately mitigate any potentially
adverse influences, including but not limited to flooding, erosion, subsidence, sloping of the soil or
other dangers and nuisances.
         c.   Building Location: All buildings shall be located to avoid, to the extent practicable, critical
lands. Building locations and associated lot configurations should be oriented to encourage active

and passive solar design principles wherever practicable. Building design should create
recognizable streets and other spaces with their edges defined by buildings, making it easy for
anyone to find their way around, and promote safety and accessibility.
         d.   Access: All MPDs shall have vehicular access from a state highway or county road. All
developments shall have a secondary point of access/emergency access unless otherwise
mitigated to the satisfaction of the Summit County engineer and fire marshal. Roads, utility lines,
and buildings should be designed to work with the existing grade. Cuts and fills should be
minimized.
         e.   Trails: Existing trails should be incorporated into the project and should be maintained in
their existing location whenever possible. Trail easements for existing trails may be required.
Construction of new transportation and/or recreational trails will be required to be consistent with
Summit County's active transportation plan and/or Snyderville Basin special recreation district's
trails master plan, respectively. All new construction will meet or exceed the design standards set
forth in the plans.
         f.   Connectivity: All MPDs shall provide a means of direct, and safe pedestrian and bicycle
linkages within the project area as well as connections to adjacent/off-site sidewalk, pathway, and
trail systems. Pedestrian/ equestrian/bicycle circulation shall be separated from vehicular
circulation wherever reasonable.
      Where applicable, MPDs will have a system of streets, alleys, and pedestrian pathways with
multiple routes and connections serving the same origins and destinations. All streets, alleys, and
pedestrian pathways shall connect to other streets and to existing and projected streets outside the
proposed MPD or other development. Dead ends or cul-de-sacs are discouraged.
         g.   Snow Removal: Snow storage shall be compliant with the requirements of chapter
10-4-14 of this title. The site plan shall include adequate areas for snow removal and snow storage.
The landscape plan shall allow for snow storage areas. Structures shall be set back from any hard
surfaces to provide adequate areas to remove and store snow. Snow should be stored on site and
not removed to an off-site location.
         h.   Waste Disposal/Recycling Facilities: All waste disposal and recycling facilities shall be
compliant with the requirements of chapter 10-4-13 of this title. The site plan shall include adequate
areas for trash dumpsters and recycling containers, including an adequate circulation area for pick-
up vehicles. These facilities shall be enclosed and shall be included on the site and landscape
plans for the project. Pedestrian access shall be provided to the refuse/recycling facilities from
within the MPD for the convenience of residents and guests.
         i.   Service And Delivery Access: Service and delivery access and loading/unloading areas
must be included in the site plan. The service and delivery should be kept separate from pedestrian
areas.
         j.   Utilities: Existing or proposed utility and public services for MPDs will be adequate to
support the proposed project at normal service levels and will be designed in a manner to avoid
adverse impacts on adjacent land uses, public services, and utility resources, unless otherwise
permitted by this chapter.
      6.   Transportation: MPDs shall include transportation approaches that add more options for
public transportation, pedestrians, and bicycle users, and reduce the need for driving. An MPD
should include:
         a.   Transportation amenities including drop-off areas for van and shuttle service, and a bus
stop, if applicable;

landscaping work to achieve this goal?
         b.   Water Quality: What measures are being taken to manage water use by the development,

surrounding water ways.
         c.   Air Quality: Explain what measures are being taken to ensure the development does not
have a negative effect on Summit County's air quality.

         i.   Landscaping: Explain how the proposed landscaping will be appropriate for the climate
and topography of the site. Explain how the irrigation system will be water wise and designed to
preserve as much water as possible.
      13.   Site Design Narrative: A MPD application shall include a written explanation of how the
project plan addresses the following design questions:
         a.   Project Neighborhood Connectivity: How does the proposed development interconnect
with the surrounding properties, neighborhood, and area? Including but not limited to:
            (1)   Where will vehicles enter and exit the site?
            (2)   Where will new streets be developed?
            (3)   How pedestrian and bicycle routes (including commuter pathways, trails, and
sidewalks) be provided through the project area?
         b.   Availability Of Neighborhood Facilities And Services: Is the location of the proposed
development within reasonable proximity (including walking and biking) to community facilities such
as schools, retail centers, parks, etc.?
         c.   Housing Needs: How does the proposed development advance the community need for a
mix of housing types and affordability?
         d.   Character: What are the design objectives for the built environment, including buildings

and the public spaces that connect them? How do these design objectives address the local
context, climate, and/or community needs?
         e.   Site Design: How is the proposed development designed to take advantage of the existing
topography, landscape features, trees, wildlife corridors, existing structures, existing waterways,
minimize site grading, etc.?
         f.   Complete Street Design: How is the proposed development street/circulation system
designed to accommodate a variety of transportation modes (where appropriate), easy route

         g.   Parking Areas: How does the proposed development balance the need for parking with

   F.   Required Findings Of Fact: The county must find enough evidence to support the following

approval to ensure compliance with these findings.
      1.   The MPD is designed to fit well into the natural terrain, minimize excessive site grading and
protect, preserve, and enhance the level of quality of the surrounding area.
      2.   The MPD makes suitable provisions for the protection, preservation, and enhancement of
watercourses, drainage areas, wooded areas, rough terrain and similar natural features and areas.
      3.   The MPD takes adjacent land uses into consideration and mitigates potential impacts,
including but not limited to flooding, erosion, subsidence, sloping of the soil or other dangers and
nuisances, through careful planning.
      4.   The MPD has direct vehicular access from a state highway or county road or suitable
private road or driveway access meeting all requirements of the county engineer and fire marshal.
      5.   The MPD has a secondary point of access/emergency access or other mitigation
satisfactory to the Summit County engineer and fire marshal.
      6.   All roads/streets within MPD follow the natural contours of the site wherever possible to
minimize the amount of grading.
      7.   Existing or proposed utility and public services are adequate to support the proposed MPD
at normal service levels and are designed in a manner to avoid adverse impacts on adjacent land
uses, public services, and utility resources.
      8.   The proposed structures within the MPD are located on the most developable and least
environmentally sensitive portions of the site. The open areas within the MPD are designed so that

STAFF REPORT 
 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Amir Caus, AICP, Senior Planner 
Date of Meeting: May 27, 2025  
Type of Item:   Low Impact Permit - Public Hearing, Possible Action  
Process:   Administrative Review 
File #:   25-049 
 
 
Proposal 
 
The applicant is proposing to construct a 13,680 sq. ft. retail/office building on Lot 5 of the 
Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision. The applicant is proposing a shell building and the 
tenants have not yet been determined.  
 
The applicant has stated that the building has been designed to “accommodate a flexible mix of 
professional office and retail tenants. It is not configured to support restaurants, which aligns 
with both parking capacity and community vision. Our intent is to attract good-credit tenants 
that provide useful services to the surrounding Pinebrook, Summit Park, and Jeremy Ranch 
neighborhoods, creating a cohesive and vibrant local center.” 
 
 
Project Description 
 
Project Name: Quarry Village Lot 5 Commercial Shell Building Low Impact Permit  
Applicant(s):   Franz Long, Prime Builders 
Property Owner(s):   WCB Holdings Inc. (William C. Berghoff) 
Location:   3167 Quarry Road, Pinebrook, Summit County, UT  
Zone District:   Rural Residential (RR) – also governed by Gorgoza Pines Consent  
    Agreement and Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Plat A 
Parcel Number and Size: QJPB-A-5-1AM (0.76 acres)  
Type of Process:   Administrative 
Final Land Use Authority: Community Development Director  
 
 
 
80
2 
 
 
Vicinity Map 
 
 
 
Background 
 
The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision was approved on May 21, 2004 and consists of 
eleven (11) lots. The subdivision was created pursuant to the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent 
Agreement, which was approved by the Summit County Board of Commissioners 
on February 24, 1997. The agreement was the result of a vested rights suit between Summit 
County and the developers of Gorgoza Pines Ranch. 
 
The subdivision was approved for ~170,000 square feet of commercial density. Several of the 
buildings have been constructed, or the lots have been subdivided and entitled 
with maximum square footage allocations. 
 
In 2003, as part of a Plat Amendment process to reconfigure the Quarry Junction Subdivision, 
the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission conditioned that all buildings in the subdivision be 
processed through the Low Impact Permit process and that the Snyderville Basin Planning 
Commission make a recommendation on architectural and landscape plans to the Community 
Development Director prior to any final approval.  
 
 
 
81
3 
 
 
Analysis and Findings 
 
The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision is governed by the Gorgoza Pines Consent 
Agreement which lists the type of uses allowed. The applicant has stated the proposed uses will 
be consistent with the Consent Agreement. 
 
The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision designates maximum gross square footage and 
maximum gross building footprints for each lot. Lot 5 is allowed 13,696 sq. ft. of gross 
building area and 6,848 sq. ft. of gross building footprint. As designed, the proposed building is 
in compliance with the square footage allotments. 
 
Low Impact Permit Review 
 
In 2003, as part of a Plat Amendment process to reconfigure the Quarry Junction Subdivision, 
the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission conditioned that all buildings in the subdivision be 
processed through the Low Impact Permit process and that the Snyderville Basin Planning 
Commission make a recommendation on architectural and landscape plans to the Community 
Development Director prior to any final approval.  
 
Consent Agreement Compliance 
 
The following development standards are identified in the Consent Agreement and are 
applicable to Lot 5. 
 
Densities and Uses: The uses and densities… shall be in accordance with the Consent 
Agreement. Parcels A and B as shown in the Consent Agreement are intended for a variety of 
retail commercial, service commercial, office uses or single or multiple family dwelling units.  
 
Analysis: Business licenses for future uses will be issued pursuant to the allowed uses 
identified in the Consent Agreement. The square footage limitations identified on the 
subdivision plat have been complied with. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
 
Setbacks: To the extent that standard setbacks cannot be met within a development, by virtue 
of such approved densities and uses, such setback requirements shall be waived or relaxed as 
long as the County reasonably determines that public safety, health, and welfare concerns can 
be adequately satisfied.  
 
Analysis: The required setbacks in the RR zone are thirty feet from the edge of the right-
of-way, fifty five (55) feet from the centerline of the road (where property line extends 
to the road) and twelve (12) feet from the side and rear property lines. The proposed 
approximate setbacks are: 
 
 
 
82
4 
 
LOT 5

~47’ from 
centerline of 
Quarry Road 
(North side) 
~12’ from the 
Pinebrook Blvd 
right-of-way 
(West side) 
11.5’ 
(East side) 
45’ 
(south side) 
 
The Quarry Village project was a master planned project and all infrastructure, including 
parking, were improved when the project was approved. The project accommodated 
compact parking areas and circulation throughout. The proposed setbacks are similar to 
the existing Quarry Village buildings and follow the master planned pattern. Service 
providers, including the Park City Fire Department and Summit County Engineering 
Department have reviewed the proposal and have not found any concerns with public 
safety, health, and welfare. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
 
Landscaping: Landscaping and maintenance requirements shall be in accordance with Summit 
County ordinances.  
 
Analysis: The applicant is proposing landscape islands in the pedestrian circulation areas 
with trees, shrubs, and perennials. Trees, shrubs, and perennials have been 
incorporated in the immediate vicinity of the building. Transitional sod has been 
incorporated on the outer edges of the property. COMPLIES 
 
Parking: Consideration should be given to limiting such parking surface area to the extent 
reasonably possible. Solutions such as on street parking and joint use parking facilities shall be 
considered.  
 
Analysis: Summit County employs a maximum parking requirement rather than a 
minimum for commercial space. Quarry Village has historically used shared parking. 
Staff historically has not recorded any complaints regarding parking conflicts. The 
parking being provided is considered adequate. COMPLIES 
 
Plazas: A number of smaller plazas… are encouraged for each specific development as it occurs. 
This could include play areas, sitting areas, or simply pleasant areas to congregate.  
Analysis: The applicant has stated; “While this building does not propose a new 
standalone plaza, it benefits from and contributes to the existing central plaza space 
within Quarry Village. This shared plaza is maintained by the development and includes 
seating, landscaping, and community connectivity. Additionally, the Lot 5 building 
features two new outdoor plaza areas: an ADA-accessible space on the west side, and a 
rooftop plaza accessible via secure interior and exterior staircases. These will be 
primarily available to building tenants but can also serve as breakout areas for small 
community events.”  While the Gorgoza Pines Consent Agreement encourages plazas, it 
does not specify whether they should be public or private. The applicant has included 
private amenity areas and is relying on shared areas provided by other projects. 
COMPLIES 
83
5 
 
Scale: Commercial development within the Project shall be of a scale that is typical of a 
traditional town. There may be locations for anchor tenants, together with significant space for 
smaller scale users.  
Analysis: The anchor tenant in the overall Quarry Village project is the existing grocery 
store. The applicant has stated that; “The building design is a modern interpretation of 
traditional Park City Main Street architecture, utilizing modules and materials that 
emphasize human scale and variety. Exterior finishes include wood siding, metal accents, 
and standing seam roofing, all carefully selected to visually integrate with the existing 
Quarry Village aesthetic. The north and south sides provide widened sidewalk zones and 
outdoor interaction areas, enhancing the pedestrian experience and supporting 
incidental gathering.” The intent of the proposed buildings is to provide spaces to 
accommodate retail and office, which are uses allowed in the Agreement. The 
applicant’s plans show sidewalk connections to the existing infrastructure along 
Pinebrook Blvd. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
Building Heights: Generally building heights will be restricted to two (2) stories. A third story is 
allowed so long as it occurs within the area formed by a pitched roof on the two (2) story 
structure where approved by Summit County. The Gorgoza Pines Consent Agreement allows 
heights to be up to 35 feet above natural or finished grade, whichever is lower.  
 
The majority of the building has been designed as a two-story structure with a ~30-foot height 
except the rooftop patio access and trellis which bring the building up to 35 feet in height. As 
drawn, the applicant is below the 35-foot maximum height allowed. COMPLIES 
 
Signage and Lighting: Outdoor signs and lighting shall be in accordance with Summit County 
ordinances.  
 
Analysis: The applicant is proposing wall packs at each entrance. The wall packs are 
directed downward and are of full cutoff variety. The applicant is also utilizing existing 
parking lot lighting. One parking lot pole is being moved to accommodate the project. 
Staff has included conditions of approval to limit the temperature and brightness of 
each exterior fixture. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED 
 
Architecture: Buildings should be designed to invite public comprehension and reflect their 
function or use. Access, when necessary, should be easily discernible and obvious without the 
necessity of excessive signage. Facades should be scaled to and designed to accommodate use 
by humans. Vehicular and equipment structure design should mask the scale of their use. 
Decorative elements may be used to enhance, but should not suggest cosmetic treatment.  
 
Analysis: The structure has been designed to accommodate a variety of users. Materials 
for the building are a mix of horizontal wood siding, vertical wood siding, metal siding, 
and stucco (EIFS). The applicant is proposing standing seam metal roofing for the sloped 
sections and a synthetic rubber membrane for the flat roof.  
 
84
6 
 
The project architect has stated that; “The mechanicals will be designed for each tenant 
after they have been determined and their mechanicals will be designed around their  
exact needs and design loads. Saying that, I refer to Detail 16 on Sheet A6.0 for a 
mechanical rooftop screen that will be used to screen mechanical equipment.  Exact 
screening cannot be determined until exact mechanicals are determined.  No on-grade 
mechanical units are anticipated.” Staff have included a condition of approval that 
requires all mechanical equipment to be screened, including future tenant 
improvements. Staff have also included conditions of approval that prohibits façade 
mechanical equipment and that if any is proposed that a Low Impact Permit 
Amendment would be required. COMPLIES AS CONDITIONED

Recommendation 
 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission review the proposed Low 
Impact Permit, conduct a public hearing, and make a positive recommendation to the 
Community Development Director to approve the application, pursuant to the following 
Findings of Fact, Conclusions of Law, and Conditions of Approval: 
 
Findings of Fact: 
 
1. The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision was approved on May 21, 2004 and 
consists of eleven (11) lots.  
2. The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision was created pursuant to the Gorgoza 
Pines Ranch Consent Agreement, which was approved by the Summit County Board of 
Commissioners on February 24, 1997.

9

between Summit County and the developers of Gorgoza Pines Ranch. 
4. The Quarry Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision was approved for ~170,000 square feet of 
commercial density.  
5. Several of the buildings have been constructed, or the lots have been subdivided and 
entitled with maximum square footage allocations. 
6. In 2003, as part of a Plat Amendment process to reconfigure the Quarry Junction 
Subdivision, the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission conditioned that all buildings in 
the subdivision be processed through the Low Impact Permit process and that the 
Snyderville Basin Planning Commission make a recommendation on architectural and

Conclusions of Law: 
 
1. The proposed development complies with the applicable standards of the Gorgoza Pines 
Ranch Consent Agreement.  
2. The proposed development complies with the applicable standards of the Quarry 
Junction at Pinebrook Subdivision.  
 
 
89
11 
 
 
3. The proposed project, as conditioned, meets the applicable standards set forth in the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code, Section 10-3-5: Conditional Use Permit. 
4. The proposed development complies with the applicable standards of the Snyderville 
Basin Development Code.   
5. The proposed development is consistent with the goals and objectives of the Snyderville 
Basin General Plan. 
 
Conditions of Approval: 
 
1. The locations, heights, and design of the proposed project shall be per Exhibit A of this 
letter. 
2. No outdoor storage is permitted. 
3. Due to lack of delivery space, large-truck deliveries that will block circulation shall be 
prohibited. 
4. Any utilization of large-truck deliveries will require an amendment to this permit to 
accommodate a redesign and incorporation of large-truck deliveries.  
5. Signage and signage relocation approvals are not included as part of this approval and 
shall be reviewed through separate sign permits. 
6. Exterior lighting temperature shall not exceed three thousand degrees (3,000°) Kelvin, 
except the parking lot light pole being replaced which may not exceed five thousand 
degrees (5,000°) Kelvin. 
7. Exterior lighting output shall not exceed two thousand (2,000) Lumens, except the 
parking lot light pole being replaced which may not exceed two thousand five hundred 
(2,500) Lumens. 
8. All disturbed areas shall be revegetated with a native, drought-tolerant seed mix or as 
per the submitted Landscape Plan included in the exhibit package. 
9. A bond shall be in place to ensure site restoration/revegetation of the disturbed areas. 
10. All necessary permits must be obtained, and fees shall be paid prior to the 
commencement of any construction activity, including but not limited to the Summit 
Building Department and Summit County Engineering Department. 
11. All other service provider requirements shall be met. 
12. Business licenses for future uses shall be issued pursuant to the allowed uses identified 
in the Gorgoza Pines Ranch Consent Agreement. 
13. Any business licenses issued that are not according to the uses identified in the Gorgoza 
Pines Ranch Consent Agreement shall be revoked.  
14. Prior to final approval of a building permit, the applicant shall submit construction plans 
to the Engineering Department in conjunction with a Development Improvement 
Agreement. 
15. All mechanical equipment, including equipment related with future tenant 
improvements, shall be screened. 
16. Façade mechanical equipment, including vents, shall be prohibited. 
17. Any façade mechanical equipment, including vents shall require a Low Impact Permit 
Amendment.  
18. No modification to the approved Low Impact Permit is permitted without the prior 
approval of the Summit County Community Development Department.

Subject: Quarry 5 Retail Building – Project Description and Submission 
I am writing to provide an overview of the proposed development for Lot 5 of Quarry Village Shopping Center, 
located at 3167 Quarry Road, Park City, Utah 84098. The project, known as Quarry 5 Retail Building, is a 13,728-
square-foot, two-story commercial structure designed for retail and business occupancy. 
The scope of the project includes: 
• Core & shell construction of the building 
• Site improvements and utilities 
• Landscaping and irrigation

building specifications. We are submitting these plans for review and approval as part of the permitting process. Please
